I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or wanting to die?\n\nPatient: Um… not like I’ve made a plan or anything. I don’t want you to think I’m about to do something. But… yeah, I guess sometimes I catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up one morning. It’s not something I’m proud of saying.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, low feeling?\n\nPatient: I guess… longer than I’d like to admit. Looking back, these low, guilty stretches have been coming and going for maybe around eleven months. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my appetite’s been kind of off. Some nights I just grab a few bites, or I skip supper and tell myself I’ll eat later. My belt’s been a bit looser, but I don’t think it’s a huge amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get things done physically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’m, like, dragging myself around all day, but by the time I get home from the office, even small stuff feels harder than it should. Like checking the mail or loading the dishwasher… I’ll look at it and it just feels like more effort than it ought to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt similarly low and disconnected from things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped enjoying everything, but stuff I usually look forward to just kind of sits there. Like grilling on the weekend, or going fishing, or even just putting on a record after work… lately I’ll think about it and then just not bother. It’s not that I hate it, I just… don’t really care the way I normally would, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low feelings or lack of interest in activities tend to happen at a specific time of year?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a pattern like that, honestly. It doesn’t seem like it’s always winter or summer or anything. I guess I haven’t really tracked it that closely, but nothing obvious comes to mind. Is that something I should be paying more attention to?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a hard time not… kind of tearing myself down. I know that may sound silly, but it’s been getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or on edge?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say often, exactly. I mean, I get tense sometimes, sure, but I don’t know if it’s more than what most people deal with. Maybe I’m on edge here and there, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing persistent feelings of sadness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days lately there’s been this kind of heavy, low feeling. Like the color’s turned down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, I still like those things in theory… grilling on the weekend, going fishing, putting on a record. Usually I’d look forward to that. Lately, though, I just kind of leave the stuff sitting there and don’t really feel much pull to do it. It’s not like I hate it now or anything, I just… don’t really care as much, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel much pleasure when you do engage in those activities you mentioned, like grilling or listening to records?\n\nPatient: Not as much as I used to, no. I mean, if I make myself put on a record or fire up the grill, it’s… fine, I guess. But I don’t really get that feeling of looking forward to it, or enjoying it the same way. A lot of the time the stuff just sits there and I don’t bother with it. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or wanting to die?\n\nPatient: Um… not like I’ve made a plan or anything. I don’t want you to think I’m about to do something. But… yeah, I guess sometimes I catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up one morning. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my appetite’s been kind of off. Some nights I just grab a few bites, or I skip supper and tell myself I’ll eat later. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped enjoying everything, but stuff I usually look forward to just kind of sits there. Like grilling on the weekend, or going fishing, or even just putting on a record after work… lately I’ll think about it and then just not bother. It hasn’t been constant every single day. But looking back, these low, guilty kinds of periods have been coming and going for… maybe about eleven months now. I don’t know if that’s the right way to describe it, but it’s been off and on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low moods might be related to a specific time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I haven’t noticed it lining up with a season or anything like that. It could be that I’m missing something, but nothing really jumps out at me as “this happens every year around the same time.”\n\nDoctor: When you have those stretches where you feel more like yourself, do they last for a while or are they pretty short-lived?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, to be honest. Some of them feel like they last a decent bit, not just a single good day, but I haven’t really kept track carefully. I guess they come and go… I don’t know if that’s specific enough.\n\nDoctor: How long do those periods where you feel more like yourself usually last?\n\nPatient: I wish I had a cleaner answer. I’d say… maybe a couple weeks sometimes? Other times shorter. It’s not like I’ve been marking it on a calendar, so I don’t want to overstate it. But usually it feels like enough time that I think, “Okay, maybe I’m getting back to normal,” and then later it kind of dips again. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your low periods and periods where you feel more like yourself follow a predictable pattern or cycle?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed a predictable cycle, no. It’s more like I realize afterward that I was in a low stretch, or that I’ve been doing a bit better for a while. I’m sorry, I know that’s not very precise… I just haven’t been able to pin it down to a pattern.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t want you to think I’m about to do something. But… yeah, I guess sometimes I catch myself thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up one morning. It’s not something I’m proud of saying.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, low feeling?\n\nPatient: I guess… longer than I’d like to admit. Looking back, these low, guilty stretches have been coming and going for maybe around eleven months. Not constant every single day, but… yeah, it’s been hanging around a while.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but my appetite’s been kind of off. Some nights I just grab a few bites, or I skip supper and tell myself I’ll eat later. My belt’s been a bit looser, but I don’t think it’s a huge amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get things done physically?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I wouldn’t say I’m, like, dragging myself around all day, but by the time I get home from the office, even small stuff feels harder than it should. Like checking the mail or loading the dishwasher… I’ll look at it and it just feels like more effort than it ought to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you felt similarly low and disconnected from things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve completely stopped enjoying everything, but stuff I usually look forward to just kind of sits there. Like grilling on the weekend, or going fishing, or even just putting on a record after work… lately I’ll think about it and then just not bother. It’s not that I hate it, I just… don’t really care the way I normally would, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low feelings or lack of interest in activities tend to happen at a specific time of year?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a pattern like that, honestly. It doesn’t seem like it’s always winter or summer or anything. I guess I haven’t really tracked it that closely, but nothing obvious comes to mind. Is that something I should be paying more attention to?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling okay or back to normal in between these low stretches over the past eleven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there have been some stretches where I felt more like myself, I guess. It hasn’t been constant every single day. But looking back, these low, guilty kinds of periods have been coming and going for… maybe about eleven months now. I don’t know if that’s the right way to describe it, but it’s been off and on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low moods might be related to a specific time of year?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I haven’t noticed it lining up with a season or anything like that. Some of them feel like they last a decent bit, not just a single good day, but I haven’t really kept track carefully.
